Let me outline a plan:

**1. Configuration options (`lite-llm/config.nix`)**
- Add a new option, e.g., `users`, which is a list (or attrset) of user definitions.
- Each user has a name (and possibly other metadata), but no key — the key is generated at runtime.
- Example: `users = lib.mkOption { type = listOf str; }` or an attrset of user names to extra config.

**2. Key generation in the launch wrapper (`lite-llm/setup.nix`)**
- The wrapper script (probably a shell script that starts litellm) needs to:
  - For each defined user, check if a key already exists in some persistent location (e.g., a state directory or a keys file).
  - If not, generate a random key (e.g., `openssl rand -hex 32` or `head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64`).
  - Store the keys persistently so they can be reused across restarts.
  - Write the keys to a file that the authenticator can read (e.g., a JSON or YAML file with user → key mappings).

**3. Custom authenticator**
- LiteLLM supports custom authentication via `general_settings.custom_auth: CustomAuth` where the authenticator is a Python class in a file in the same directory as the config YAML.
- The authenticator class needs a method like `auth(self, credentials: str) -> Optional[Union[LiteLLM_UserTable, LiteLLM_VerificationToken]]` or for the newer versions, `async def auth(self, request, data, response)`.
- The authenticator will load the user/key pairs (from the file generated by the wrapper) and check if the request's key matches a known user's key.
- The Python file needs to be written to the same directory as the YAML config at runtime (since the config directory is likely generated at runtime in a runtime dir).

**4. Runtime configuration generation (`lite-llm/setup.nix`)**
- The YAML config generated for litellm must include `general_settings.custom_auth: <authenticator module name>` to enable the authenticator.
- The authenticator Python file must be placed in the same directory as the YAML config.

Let me think about LiteLLM's custom auth mechanism more concretely:

In LiteLLM's config.yaml:
```yaml
general_settings:
  master_key: sk-...
  custom_auth: custom_auth.CustomAuth  # file custom_auth.py with class CustomAuth
```

The custom auth file must be importable from the directory where litellm is started / where the config is. LiteLLM loads it via `get_instance_fn` which imports the module path relative to the config directory.

The CustomAuth class:
```python
from litellm import CustomLogger
from litellm.proxy._types import LiteLLM_UserTable, LiteLLM_VerificationToken

class CustomAuth(CustomLogger):
    def auth(self, credentials: str) -> Optional[Union[LiteLLM_UserTable, LiteLLM_VerificationToken]]:
        # check credentials
        ...
```

Actually in newer LiteLLM versions, custom auth uses:
```python
class MyCustomHandler(CustomLogger):
    async def async_pre_call_check(self, request: Request, data: dict) -> Optional[Union[LiteLLM_UserTable, LiteLLM_VerificationToken]]:
        ...
```

1. `general_settings.custom_auth` — points to a class with an `auth` method (older approach).
2. `general_settings.custom_auth` with `async_pre_call_check` (newer approach).

Here's a plan based on the task description. Note that I haven't seen the contents of `lite-llm/config.nix` or `lite-llm/setup.nix` yet, so some details are assumptions — I can refine this once you add those files to the chat.

## Plan

### 1. User options in `lite-llm/config.nix`
- Add a `users` option to the configuration interface, e.g. a list of usernames (or an attrset if users need extra attributes later).
- Deliberately **no key field** — keys are runtime-generated, not configuration.

### 2. Key generation in the launch wrapper (`lite-llm/setup.nix`)
- In the wrapper script that starts litellm, before launching:
  - Pick a **persistent location** for credentials (e.g. a state directory or a `keys.json` file next to the runtime config).
  - For each configured user, check whether a key already exists in that file; if not, generate a random one (e.g. `openssl rand -hex 32`).
  - **Re-use existing keys** — the file is only appended to, never regenerated, so keys survive restarts.
  - Write the resulting user→key mapping to a file the authenticator can read (JSON is convenient).

### 3. Custom authenticator
- Create a Python module (e.g. `custom_auth.py`) containing a litellm custom auth class.
- The class loads the user/key mapping produced by the wrapper and validates incoming requests: it checks the `Authorization: Bearer <key>` credential against the known keys and resolves the matching user; unknown keys are rejected.
- Since litellm imports the authenticator by module path, the `.py` file must be **placed in the same directory as the runtime YAML config**. Two options:
  - Write it from the wrapper at startup, or
  - Ship it as a Nix store file and copy/symlink it into the runtime config dir.

### 4. Enable the authenticator in the runtime config
- In the YAML generated by `setup.nix`, add under `general_settings`:
  ```yaml
  general_settings:
    custom_auth: custom_auth.CustomAuth
  ```
  (exact module/class path depends on the litellm version's expected interface — `auth(credentials)` vs. `async_pre_call_check`).
- Ensure the wrapper creates the runtime config directory, writes the YAML, the authenticator `.py`, and the keys file there, then starts litellm pointing at that YAML.

### 5. Verification
- Build the module, start the wrapper twice to confirm keys are stable across restarts.
- Send a request with a valid key (accepted) and an invalid key (rejected).

## Open questions to resolve once I see the files
- How the runtime config directory is currently created/managed in `setup.nix` (temp dir vs. persistent state dir) — this determines where keys can persist.
- Which litellm version is used, to pick the right custom-auth API.
- Whether the wrapper is a shell script or a Nix-generated script, and how the YAML is currently templated.

Key design decisions:

def get_custom_auth(class_name: Optional[str]):
    if class_name is None:
        return None
    module_name = "custom_auth"
    if "." in class_name:
        module_name = class_name.split(".")[0]
        class_name = class_name.split(".")[1]
    module = importlib.import_module(module_name)
    if class_name is None:
        return module
    class_object = getattr(module, class_name)
    instance = class_object()
    return instance.auth
```

Let me write the concrete plan:

## 1. `lite-llm/config.nix` — add `users` option

```nix
users = mkOption {
  type = types.listOf types.str;
  default = [];
  description = ''
    Users allowed to authenticate against this lite-llm instance.
    The authentication keys are generated at runtime by the launch
    script and are not part of this configuration.
  '';
};
```

## 2. `lite-llm/setup.nix` — authenticator module

Generate `custom_auth.py` per instance (embeds the configured user names, reads keys from a runtime dir):

```nix
custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
  import os

  USERS = [ ${lib.concatStringsSep ", " (map (u: ''"${u}"'') lite-llm.users)} ]

  class CustomAuth:
      def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
          users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
          for user in USERS:
              try:
                  with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as f:
                      if f.read().strip() == token:
                          return True
              except OSError:
                  continue
          return False
'';
```

Hmm, string escaping of usernames — use `lib.strings.toJSON` or `builtins.toJSON` for the list: `USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}` — much cleaner! Yes: `USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}` produces valid Python for a list of strings. 

## 3. Config YAML + same-directory requirement

- Add `general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.CustomAuth"` to `lite-llm-config` (only when users non-empty).
- Replace single-file `lite-llm-config-yml` with a directory derivation containing both `config.yml` and `custom_auth.py`:

```nix
lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
  mkdir -p "$out"
  cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
  cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
'';
```

- Launch script: `--config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml"`.

## 4. Key generation in the launch wrapper

After sourcing get-config and before api-key-setup (or after), add:

```bash
users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
mkdir -p "$users_dir"
for user in <escaped user list>; do
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/$user.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/$user.key"
  fi
done
export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
```

Keys persist in DEVTOOLS_CONFIG → reused across launches. Only generate when users non-empty (guard in nix: `lib.optionalString (lite-llm.users != [])`).

## 5. Verification

- `nix build` / run the app; check `$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users/*.key` created; restart → same keys.
- Request without/with wrong key → 401; with correct key → proxied.
- Check litellm logs for custom_auth import.

Now that I can see the actual code, here's a concrete plan. The current design has one important constraint: `lite-llm-config-yml` is a single `pkgs.writeText` file in the store, but the task requires the authenticator to live **next to** the YAML — so the config output has to become a *directory* containing both files.

## Design summary

- Keys are runtime state, stored under the tool's private config dir (`$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG`), mirroring how provider API keys are already handled.
- The authenticator is a static, per-instance Python file generated by Nix. It embeds the configured user list and reads the keys from a directory pointed to by an env var exported by the wrapper.
- The wrapper generates a random key per user **only if the key file doesn't exist yet** → keys are reused across launches.
- If no users are configured, no auth is enabled (current behavior preserved).

## 1. `lite-llm/config.nix` — add `users` option

Add to `spec`:

```nix
users = mkOption {
  type = types.listOf types.str;
  default = [];
  description = ''
    Users allowed to authenticate against this lite-llm instance.
    Keys are generated at launch time and are not part of the config.
  '';
};
```

## 2. `lite-llm/setup.nix` — four changes

**a. Generate the authenticator** (`custom_auth.py`, per instance, embedding the user list):

```nix
custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
  import os
  USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}
  class CustomAuth:
      def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
          ...
'';
```

`auth` checks the bearer token against each `$LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR/<user>.key` file, returns `True` on match. Embedding `USERS` means removing a user from the config revokes their access even if their key file lingers.

**b. Enable it in the YAML** — extend `lite-llm-config`:

```nix
lite-llm-config = {
  model_list = ...;
} // lib.optionalAttrs (lite-llm.users != []) {
  general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.CustomAuth";
};
```

**c. Bundle YAML + authenticator into one directory** (replaces the bare `writeText` as the thing the wrapper points at):

```nix
lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
  mkdir -p "$out"
  cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
  cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
'';
```

and change the launch line to `--config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml"`.

**d. Key generation in the wrapper**, after sourcing `get-config`:

```bash
users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
mkdir -p "$users_dir"
for user in ${lib.concatStringsSep " " (map lib.escapeShellArg lite-llm.users)}; do
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/$user.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/$user.key"
  fi
done
export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
```

(emit this block only when `users != []`). Exporting a dedicated env var (rather than relying on `DEVTOOLS_CONFIG` being exported) guarantees the Python process can find the keys.

## 3. Verification

- Launch twice; confirm `$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users/*.key` are created once and unchanged on relaunch.
- Request with no/wrong key → 401; with a key from the file → proxied successfully.

3. **Interpretation check**: with litellm's bool-returning custom auth, the *key alone* identifies the user (each user has a unique key).   custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}

    class CustomAuth:
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as f:
                        if f.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure each configured user has
  an authentication key. Keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user. A key is generated only if the file
  does not exist yet, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */

    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup lite-llm.users)}

    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;
```

Now — the authenticator Python. Let me write it carefully:

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates requests against
  the users defined in the configuration. The class checks the
  bearer token of the request against the key files generated by
  the launch script, one per user, located in the directory
  pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}

    class CustomAuth:
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                key_file = os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")
                try:
                    with open(key_file) as f:
                        if f.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. Keys live in the tool's configuration directory,
  one file per user, under the directory pointed to by the
  'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable. The variable is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}

    class CustomAuth:
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

from fastapi import HTTPException, Request

def custom_auth(auth_str: str) -> bool:

Let me write the setup.nix changes as a coherent set, showing where each piece goes:

**a. authenticator** (new binding, after `to-model-config` / before or after `lite-llm-config`):

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token of
  each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. Keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, in the directory pointed to by the
  'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is exported by
  the launch script.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}

    class CustomAuth:
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

**b. YAML** (modify `lite-llm-config`):

```nix
  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (lite-llm.users != [ ]) {
    # litellm resolves the module relative to the directory that
    # contains the yaml configuration.
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.CustomAuth";
  };
```

**c. config dir** (replaces direct use of `lite-llm-config-yml` in the launch line):

```nix
  /**
  litellm imports the custom authenticator as a python module located
  next to the yaml configuration, so both files are bundled in a
  single directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';
```

**d. key setup** (new bindings, near `to-api-key-setup`):

```nix
  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that every user defined in
  the configuration has an authentication key. Keys are stored in the
  tool's configuration directory, one file per user. A key is
  generated only if its file does not exist yet, so keys are re-used
  across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user: ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  '';

  users-setup =
    if lite-llm.users == [ ]
    then ""
    else ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup lite-llm.users)}
    '';
```

**e. launch script** — add `${users-setup}` and point `--config` at the dir:

```nix
    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
```

Here's the implementation:

## 1. `lite-llm/config.nix` — add `users` to `spec`

```nix
      users = mkOption {
        type = types.listOf (types.strMatching "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*");
        default = [ ];
        description = ''
          The users that are allowed to authenticate against this lite-llm
          instance. Authentication keys are generated when the instance is
          launched and are stored in the tool's private configuration
          directory; they are not part of this configuration. User names
          are used as file names for the keys, so they are restricted to
          file-name-safe characters.
        '';
      };
```

## 2. `lite-llm/setup.nix` — five changes

**a. Authenticator** (new binding, near `to-model-config`):

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token of
  each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. Keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON lite-llm.users}

    class CustomAuth:
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

Embedding `USERS` means removing a user from the config revokes their access even if their key file lingers in `private/`.

**b. Enable it in the YAML** (extend `lite-llm-config`):

```nix
  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (lite-llm.users != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.CustomAuth";
  };
```

**c. Bundle YAML + authenticator in one directory** (litellm resolves the module relative to the config file's directory):

```nix
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';
```

**d. Key generation** (new bindings next to `to-api-key-setup`):

```nix
  to-user-key-setup = user: ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  '';

  users-setup =
    if lite-llm.users == [ ]
    then ""
    else ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup lite-llm.users)}
    '';
```

**e. Launch script** — add `${users-setup}` after `${api-key-setup}` (and a step 3 for user keys in its doc comment), and change the config path:

```nix
    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
```

## 3. Example + verification

```nix

      enable = true;
      users = [ "alice" ];
      models = { ... };

```

2. `curl` the proxy without a key → 401; with `Authorization: Bearer $(< .../users/alice.key)` → proxied request.

No secrets reach the store: the bundle only contains `config.yml` and `custom_auth.py`; keys stay under the prelude's gitignored `private/` tree.

Actually, in litellm, `general_settings.custom_auth: "custom_auth.my_auth"` — the value is a module path + class/function name. The proxy does:

```python
custom_auth_config = general_settings.get("custom_auth", None)
if custom_auth_config:
    module, attr = custom_auth_config.rsplit(".", 1)
    mod = importlib.import_module(module)
    obj = getattr(mod, attr)
    if inspect.isclass(obj):
        custom_auth = obj()
    else:
        custom_auth = obj
```

```python
import os
from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

USERS = [...]

class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
    def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not token or not users_dir:
            return False
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                        return True
            except OSError:
                continue
        return False

### `lite-llm/config.nix`

  user-spec = types.submodule {

Or just `types.submodule {}`. Both fine. I'll write `types.submodule { options = { }; }` for clarity... Actually simpler: `user-spec = types.submodule { };`. ```nix
      users = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf user-spec;
        default = { };
        description = ''
          The users that are allowed to authenticate against this
          lite-llm instance, keyed by user name. Authentication keys
          are generated when the instance is launched and are stored
          in the tool's private configuration directory; they are not
          part of this configuration. User names are used as file

          characters.

### `lite-llm/setup.nix`

Changes:

1. `user-names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;`

2. Authenticator:

```nix
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        """
        Authenticates requests by matching the bearer token against
        the key of one of the users defined in the configuration.
        """
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

Wait — should I embed USERS or just read all `.key` files in the dir? Embedding the configured user list means removed users are revoked. Keep embedding. With attrset users, `builtins.toJSON (lib.attrNames lite-llm.users)`.

Hmm, one thing: `builtins.toJSON` on a list of strings produces `["alice"]` — valid Python. Good.

3. YAML:

```nix
  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (lite-llm.users != { }) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.UserKeyAuth";
  };
```

4. Config dir bundle — unchanged from before:

```nix
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';
```

5. Key generation:

```nix
  to-user-key-setup = user: ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  '';

  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated keys, so they are
  validated to be file-name-safe.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;

custom-auth:

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token of
  each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. It subclasses litellm's 'CustomAuth' base class,
  which is the supported way to implement custom authentication in
  litellm 1.89.0. Keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script. Only the users listed here are
  accepted, so removing a user from the configuration revokes
  their access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

```nix
  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (lite-llm.users != { }) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.UserKeyAuth";
  };
```

    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  '';

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure each configured user has
  an authentication key. Keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory and are generated only if they do not exist yet, so they
  are re-used across launches.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;
```

```

All four remarks are incorporated. Here's the final shape of the changes:

## 1. `lite-llm/config.nix` — users as objects

Users become an attrset of submodules. The submodule is empty for now, but gives per-user options (rate limits, etc.) a place to land later without changing the configuration shape:

```nix
  # This submodule configures a user that is allowed to
  # authenticate against the llm-lite instance. No options are
  # defined yet; the submodule exists so that per-user settings
  # (e.g. rate limits) can be added later. The authentication key
  # is deliberately not an option: keys are generated at launch
  # time and never enter the nix store.
  user-spec = types.submodule { options = { }; };
```

and inside `spec`:

```nix
      users = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf user-spec;
        default = { };
        description = ''
          The users that are allowed to authenticate against this
          lite-llm instance, keyed by user name. Authentication keys
          are generated when the instance is launched and are stored
          in the tool's private configuration directory; they are not
          part of this configuration. User names are used as file
          names for the keys, so they must be file-name-safe.
        '';
      };
```

## 2. `lite-llm/setup.nix`

**a. User names** (attrset keys drive everything; validated since they become filenames):

```nix
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;
```

**b. Authenticator** — subclasses litellm's `CustomAuth` base class, the supported custom-auth interface in 1.89.0 (the proxy instantiates the class and calls `auth(token)`, bool return = pass/fail):

```nix
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

Only names are embedded today; when per-user options exist, they can flow into this module the same way. Embedding the names means removing a user revokes their access even if the key file lingers.

**c. YAML** — enable the authenticator only when users are configured:

```nix
  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (lite-llm.users != { }) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.UserKeyAuth";
  };
```

**d. Config bundle** (authenticator must sit next to the YAML; litellm resolves the module relative to the config file's directory):

```nix
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';
```

**e. Key generation** — create-once semantics, so keys are reused across launches:

```nix
  to-user-key-setup = user: ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  '';

  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;
```

**f. Launch script** — insert `${users-setup}` after `${api-key-setup}` (add a step 3 for user keys to its doc comment) and point at the bundle:

```nix
    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
```

## 3. Example (`examples/flakeModule.nix`)

```nix
    config.development-tools.lite-llm.prelude-lite-llm = {
      enable = true;
      users = {
        alice = { };
      };
      models = {
        "master" = {
          model = "openrouter/mistralai/devstral";
        };
      };
    };

Original:
```nix
# This module allows configuring instances of llm lite
top@{ self, lib, config, flake-parts-lib, ... }:
let
  inherit (lib) types mkOption;
  inherit (flake-parts-lib) mkPerSystemOption;

  # This submodule will be used
  # to configure the settings of the
  # modules that the llm-lite configuration
  # will be serving.
  model-spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      model = mkOption {
        type = types.str;
        description = ''
          The name of the model. This formatter as '<provider>/<model vendor>/model'. As
          an example 'openrouter/openai/gpt-4'.
        '';
      };
    };
  };
  spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      enable = mkOption {
        type = types.bool;
        description = ''
          Enable the configuration. This adds an item to the
          set of devtools available to the flake.
        '';
      };

      models = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf model-spec;
        description = ''
          Holds the configuration of all the models that will be available
          to the llmlite instance.
        '';
      };

      port = mkOption {
        type = types.int;
        description = ''
          The port on which lite-llm will be served.
        '';
        default = 4000;
      };
    };
  };
  perSystem = _:
  let
    imports = lib.attrValues (
      lib.mapAttrs to-lite-llm top.config.development-tools.lite-llm
    );
    to-lite-llm = lite-llm-name: lite-llm: {
      imports = [ ./setup.nix ];
      config._module.args = { inherit lite-llm lite-llm-name; };
    };
  in
    { inherit imports; }
  ;
in
  {
    options = {
      development-tools.lite-llm = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf spec;
        description = ''
          This property allows configuring instances of llm-lite. These instances
          will be added to the catalogue of tools that can be run through
          the flake.
        '';
        default = {};
      };

      perSystem = mkPerSystemOption perSystem;
    };
  }
```

Original:
```nix
/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-yml' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-yml}" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}
```

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. The keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

```nix
# This module allows configuring instances of llm lite
top@{ self, lib, config, flake-parts-lib, ... }:
let
  inherit (lib) types mkOption;
  inherit (flake-parts-lib) mkPerSystemOption;

  # This submodule will be used
  # to configure the settings of the
  # modules that the llm-lite configuration
  # will be serving.
  model-spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      model = mkOption {
        type = types.str;
        description = ''
          The name of the model. This formatter as '<provider>/<model vendor>/model'. As
          an example 'openrouter/openai/gpt-4'.
        '';
      };
    };
  };

  # This submodule configures a user that is allowed to
  # authenticate against the llm-lite instance. No options are
  # defined yet; the submodule exists so that per-user settings
  # (e.g. rate limits) can be added later. The authentication key
  # is deliberately not an option: keys are generated when the
  # instance is launched and never enter the nix store.
  user-spec = types.submodule {
    options = { };
  };

  spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      enable = mkOption {
        type = types.bool;
        description = ''
          Enable the configuration. This adds an item to the
          set of devtools available to the flake.
        '';
      };

      models = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf model-spec;
        description = ''
          Holds the configuration of all the models that will be available
          to the llmlite instance.
        '';
      };

      users = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf user-spec;
        default = {};
        description = ''
          The users that are allowed to authenticate against this
          llm-lite instance, keyed by user name. Authentication keys
          are generated when the instance is launched and are stored
          in the tool's private configuration directory; they are not
          part of this configuration. User names are used as file
          names for the keys, so they must be file-name-safe.
        '';
      };

      port = mkOption {
        type = types.int;
        description = ''
          The port on which lite-llm will be served.
        '';
        default = 4000;
      };
    };
  };
  perSystem = _:
  let
    imports = lib.attrValues (
      lib.mapAttrs to-lite-llm top.config.development-tools.lite-llm
    );
    to-lite-llm = lite-llm-name: lite-llm: {
      imports = [ ./setup.nix ];
      config._module.args = { inherit lite-llm lite-llm-name; };
    };
  in
    { inherit imports; }
  ;
in
  {
    options = {
      development-tools.lite-llm = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf spec;
        description = ''
          This property allows configuring instances of llm-lite. These instances
          will be added to the catalogue of tools that can be run through
          the flake.
        '';
        default = {};
      };

      perSystem = mkPerSystemOption perSystem;
    };
  }
```

Hmm, one issue: `user-spec = types.submodule { options = { }; };` — an empty submodule. Is that valid? Yes, `types.submodule { options = {}; }` is valid; users would write `alice = {};`. Good.

Wait, actually there's a subtlety with `types.submodule` and empty options — when a user writes `users.alice = {}`, the submodule merges fine. But what if a user writes `users.alice = null`? Type error. Fine.

Hmm, but actually — will `types.submodule { options = { }; }` cause issues with `mkOption` inside? No. It's fine.

Actually, let me reconsider: should I use `types.submodule {}`? The argument to submodule is a module. `{ options = {}; }` is a module with empty options. `{}` is also a module (empty). Both work. I'll write `types.submodule { options = { }; }` for explicitness.

Now the full `setup.nix`:

```nix
/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. The keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts. Only the
  user names are embedded in the module; this ensures that
  removing a user from the configuration revokes their access
  even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.UserKeyAuth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory bundling the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the
  custom authenticator relative to the directory containing the
  yaml configuration, so both files must reside in the same
  directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user: ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  '';

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. Keys are
      generated randomly if they do not exist yet and
      are re-used if they were generated previously. The
      directory holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}
```

Now let me assemble the complete `setup.nix` file. I must be careful to include EVERYTHING from the original plus modifications.

Full setup.nix:

```nix
/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. The keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts. Only the
  user names are embedded in the module. This ensures that
  removing a user from the configuration revokes their access
  even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.UserKeyAuth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory that bundles the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the custom
  authenticator relative to the directory that contains the yaml
  configuration, so both files need to reside in the same directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user:
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and
  no authentication is enabled.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. A random
      key is generated for users that do not have one
      yet; existing keys are re-used. The directory
      holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}
```

```python
async def custom_auth_check(request: Request, api_key: str) -> UserAPIKeyAuth:
    parent_otel_span = _get_parent_otel_span_from_request(request)
    try:
        custom_auth = litellm.custom_auth
        if isinstance(custom_auth, str):
            module = importlib.import_module(custom_auth)
            instance = module.CustomAuth()
            response = instance.auth(token=api_key)
        else:
            response = custom_auth.auth(token=api_key)
        if response is False or not response:
            raise Exception("Invalid api key")
    except Exception:
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,
            detail={"error": "Invalid api key"},
        )
```

```nix
# This module allows configuring instances of llm lite
top@{ self, lib, config, flake-parts-lib, ... }:
let
  inherit (lib) types mkOption;
  inherit (flake-parts-lib) mkPerSystemOption;

  # This submodule will be used
  # to configure the settings of the
  # modules that the llm-lite configuration
  # will be serving.
  model-spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      model = mkOption {
        type = types.str;
        description = ''
          The name of the model. This formatter as '<provider>/<model vendor>/model'. As
          an example 'openrouter/openai/gpt-4'.
        '';
      };
    };
  };

  # This submodule configures a user that is allowed
  # to authenticate against the llm-lite instance. No
  # options are defined yet; the submodule exists so that
  # per-user settings (e.g. rate limits) can be added
  # later without changing the shape of the configuration.
  # The authentication key is deliberately not an option:
  # keys are generated when the instance is launched and
  # never enter the nix store.
  user-spec = types.submodule {
    options = {};
  };
  spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      enable = mkOption {
        type = types.bool;
        description = ''
          Enable the configuration. This adds an item to the
          set of devtools available to the flake.
        '';
      };

      models = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf model-spec;
        description = ''
          Holds the configuration of all the models that will be available
          to the llmlite instance.
        '';
      };

      users = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf user-spec;
        default = {};
        description = ''
          The users that are allowed to authenticate against this
          llm-lite instance, keyed by user name. Authentication keys
          are generated when the instance is launched and are stored
          in the tool's private configuration directory; they are not
          part of this configuration. User names are used as file names
          for the keys, so they must be file-name-safe. Authentication
          is enabled only when at least one user is defined.
        '';
      };

      port = mkOption {
        type = types.int;
        description = ''
          The port on which lite-llm will be served.
        '';
        default = 4000;
      };
    };
  };
  perSystem = _:
  let
    imports = lib.attrValues (
      lib.mapAttrs to-lite-llm top.config.development-tools.lite-llm
    );
    to-lite-llm = lite-llm-name: lite-llm: {
      imports = [ ./setup.nix ];
      config._module.args = { inherit lite-llm lite-llm-name; };
    };
  in
    { inherit imports; }
  ;
in
  {
    options = {
      development-tools.lite-llm = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf spec;
        description = ''
          This property allows configuring instances of llm-lite. These instances
          will be added to the catalogue of tools that can be run through
          the flake.
        '';
        default = {};
      };

      perSystem = mkPerSystemOption perSystem;
    };
  }
```

Implementing the plan now. Three files change:

1. **`lite-llm/config.nix`** — adds a `users` option: an attrset of per-user submodules (empty for now, ready for future options like rate limits). Keys are deliberately not configurable.
2. **`lite-llm/setup.nix`** — validates user names (they become file names), generates the `custom_auth.py` authenticator (subclassing litellm 1.89's `CustomAuth`), enables it in the YAML via `general_settings.custom_auth` when users are defined, bundles YAML + authenticator into one directory (litellm resolves the module relative to the config file), and generates create-once random keys in `$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users/` before launch.
3. **`examples/flakeModule.nix`** — adds an example user to the `prelude-lite-llm` instance.

lite-llm/config.nix

# This module allows configuring instances of llm lite
top@{ self, lib, config, flake-parts-lib, ... }:
let
  inherit (lib) types mkOption;
  inherit (flake-parts-lib) mkPerSystemOption;

  # This submodule will be used
  # to configure the settings of the
  # modules that the llm-lite configuration
  # will be serving.
  model-spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      model = mkOption {
        type = types.str;
        description = ''
          The name of the model. This formatter as '<provider>/<model vendor>/model'. As
          an example 'openrouter/openai/gpt-4'.
        '';
      };
    };
  };

  # This submodule configures a user that is allowed
  # to authenticate against the llm-lite instance. No
  # options are defined yet; the submodule exists so that
  # per-user settings (e.g. rate limits) can be added
  # later without changing the shape of the configuration.
  # The authentication key is deliberately not an option:
  # keys are generated when the instance is launched and
  # never enter the nix store.
  user-spec = types.submodule {
    options = {};
  };
  spec = types.submodule {
    options = {
      enable = mkOption {
        type = types.bool;
        description = ''
          Enable the configuration. This adds an item to the
          set of devtools available to the flake.
        '';
      };

      models = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf model-spec;
        description = ''
          Holds the configuration of all the models that will be available
          to the llmlite instance.
        '';
      };

      users = mkOption {
        type = types.attrsOf user-spec;
        default = {};
        description = ''
          The users that are allowed to authenticate against this
          llm-lite instance, keyed by user name. Authentication keys
          are generated when the instance is launched and are stored
          in the tool's private configuration directory; they are not
          part of this configuration. User names are used as file names
          for the keys, so they must be file-name-safe. Authentication
          is enabled only when at least one user is defined.
        '';
      };

      port = mkOption {
        type = types.int;
        description = ''
          The port on which lite-llm will be served.
        '';
        default = 4000;
      };
    };
  };
  perSystem = _:
  let
    imports = lib.attrValues (
      lib.mapAttrs to-lite-llm top.config.development-tools.lite-llm
    );
    to-lite-llm = lite-llm-name: lite-llm: {
      imports = [ ./setup.nix ];
      config._module.args = { inherit lite-llm lite-llm-name; };
    };
  in
    { inherit imports; }

in
