lite-llm/setup.nix
```
/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. The keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts. Only the
  user names are embedded in the module. This ensures that
  removing a user from the configuration revokes their access
  even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from litellm.integrations.custom_auth import CustomAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuth):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.UserKeyAuth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory that bundles the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the custom
  authenticator relative to the directory that contains the yaml
  configuration, so both files need to reside in the same directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user:
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and
  no authentication is enabled.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. A random
      key is generated for users that do not have one
      yet; existing keys are re-used. The directory
      holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}
```

```
{ config, lib, ... }:
let
  ibl-colors = [
    { name = "RainbowRed"; fg = "#E06C75"; }
    { name = "RainbowYellow"; fg ="#E5C07B"; }
    { name = "RainbowBlue"; fg = "#61AFEF"; }
  ];
in
  {
    # Enable neovim using the 'prelude' preset.
    # Use the name 'prelude-nvim' for this setup
    config.development-tools.presets.prelude.neovim.prelude-nvim.enable = true;

    # Customize the telescope exclusions of the
    # 'prelude-nvim' setup
    config.development-tools.neovim.prelude-nvim = {
      plugins.telescope.globals.file-ignore-patterns = ["flake.lock"];
    };


    config.development-tools.llm-netogallo.prelude-llm = {
      enable = true;
      task-branch-prefix = "feature";
    };

    config.development-tools.neovim.neovim-example1 = {
      enable = true;
      plugins.telescope = {
        enable = true;
        globals.file-ignore-patterns = ["flake.lock"];
      };
      plugins.indent-blankline = {
        enable = true;
        colors = ibl-colors;
      };
      keymaps = with config.development-tools.neovim.neovim-example1.commands.telescope; [
        {
          mode = "n";
          keymap = "<leader>ff";
          action = find-files;
          description = "Find files with telescope";
        }
        {
          mode = "n";
          keymap = "<leader>fg";
          action = live-grep;
          description = "Live grep search using telescope";
        }
      ];
    };

    config.development-tools.lite-llm.prelude-lite-llm = {
      enable = true;
      users = {
        alice = {};
      };
      models = {
        "master" = {
          model = "openrouter/mistralai/devstral";
        };
      };
    };
  }
```

**Verification steps:**
1. Launch `prelude-lite-llm` twice — `$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users/alice.key` should be created on the first launch (64 hex chars, mode 600) and remain untouched on the second.
2. `curl` the proxy without a key or with a wrong key → 401; with `Authorization: Bearer $(< $DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users/alice.key)` → request is proxied.
3. Remove `alice` from `users`, rebuild and relaunch — her lingering key file is ignored (401), since only names embedded in the authenticator are accepted.

```python
def get_instance_fn(value, config_file_path=None):
    module_name = value
    instance_name = None
    if "." in value:
        module_name, instance_name = value.rsplit(".", 1)
    if config_file_path is not None:
        directory = os.path.dirname(config_file_path)
        module_file_path = os.path.join(directory, *module_name.split(".")) + ".py"
        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(module_name, module_file_path)
    ...
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    if instance_name:
        instance = getattr(module, instance_name)
        if inspect.isclass(instance):
            return instance()
        return instance
    ...
```

The traceback shows `spec.loader.exec_module(module)` failing at line 3 of custom_auth.py — the import of `litellm.integrations.custom_auth`. So the module loading mechanism works (it found our file), just our import is wrong.

```python
def get_instance_fn(value, config_file_path=None):
    module_name = value
    instance_name = None
    if "." in value:
        module_name, instance_name = value.rsplit(".", 1)
    if config_file_path is not None:
        directory = os.path.dirname(config_file_path)
        module_file_path = os.path.join(directory, *module_name.split(".")) + ".py"
        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(module_name, module_file_path)
    elif os.path.isfile(os.path.join(*module_name.split(".")) + ".py"):
        ...
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    if instance_name:
        instance = getattr(module, instance_name)
        if inspect.isclass(instance):
            return instance()
        return instance
    return module
```

Let me write the final Nix binding with good comments (matching the file's doc-comment style):

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. The keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts. Only the
  user names are embedded in the module. This ensures that
  removing a user from the configuration revokes their access
  even if their key file still exists.

  The authenticator subclasses litellm's 'CustomAuth' base class
  when one can be found. The module defining the class has moved
  between litellm releases (it is not at
  'litellm.integrations.custom_auth' in litellm 1.89.0), so the
  base class is resolved at runtime from the locations it is known
  to reside in. litellm invokes the authenticator through its
  'auth' method, so a plain class is used as a fallback when no
  base class is available. This keeps the authenticator working
  across litellm version bumps in nixpkgs.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import importlib
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def find_custom_auth_base():
        for module_name in (
            "litellm.integrations.custom_auth",
            "litellm",
            "litellm.integrations.custom_logger",
            "litellm.proxy.auth.custom_auth",
        ):
            try:
                module = importlib.import_module(module_name)
            except ImportError:
                continue
            base = getattr(module, "CustomAuth", None)
            if base is not None:
                return base
        return object

The traceback pinpoints it: in litellm 1.89.0 the `CustomAuth` base class is no longer at `litellm.integrations.custom_auth` — that module path is from an older litellm layout, and the class has moved in newer releases. Note the proxy itself got as far as `get_instance_fn` loading our file, so the `custom_auth` setting and the module-loading mechanism work fine; only our import is wrong.

The base class isn't needed for the auth logic itself — litellm instantiates the class and calls its `auth` method. So the robust fix is to resolve the base class at runtime from its known locations and fall back to a plain class. That also protects the flake against litellm reshuffles on future nixpkgs bumps.

Replace the `custom-auth` binding in `lite-llm/setup.nix`:

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. The keys are stored in the tool's configuration
  directory, one file per user, under the directory pointed to by
  the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which is
  exported by the launch script before litellm starts. Only the
  user names are embedded in the module. This ensures that
  removing a user from the configuration revokes their access
  even if their key file still exists.

  The authenticator subclasses litellm's 'CustomAuth' base class
  when one can be found. The module defining that class has moved
  between litellm releases (it does not exist at
  'litellm.integrations.custom_auth' in litellm 1.89.0), so the
  base class is resolved at runtime from the locations it is known
  to reside in. litellm invokes the authenticator through its
  'auth' method, so a plain class is used as a fallback when no
  base class is available. This keeps the authenticator working
  across litellm version bumps in nixpkgs.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import importlib
    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def find_custom_auth_base():
        for module_name in (
            "litellm.integrations.custom_auth",
            "litellm",
            "litellm.integrations.custom_logger",
            "litellm.proxy.auth.custom_auth",
        ):
            try:
                module = importlib.import_module(module_name)
            except ImportError:
                continue
            base = getattr(module, "CustomAuth", None)
            if base is not None:
                return base
        return object

    CustomAuthBase = find_custom_auth_base()

    class UserKeyAuth(CustomAuthBase):
        def auth(self, token: str) -> bool:
            users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
            if not token or not users_dir:
                return False
            for user in USERS:
                try:
                    with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                        if key_file.read().strip() == token:
                            return True
                except OSError:
                    continue
            return False
  '';
```

Nothing else changes — the YAML still points at `custom_auth.UserKeyAuth`.

```python
import os
from typing import Union

from fastapi import Request

from litellm.proxy._types import UserAPIKeyAuth

USERS = [...]

async def user_api_key_auth(request: Request, api_key: str) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
    users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
    if not api_key or not users_dir:
        raise Exception("Invalid API key")
    for user in USERS:
        try:
            with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                    return api_key
        except OSError:
            continue
    raise Exception("Invalid API key")
```

import os
from typing import Union

from fastapi import Request

from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

USERS = ["alice"]

def _invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
    return ProxyException(
        message="Invalid API key",
        type="invalid_request_error",
        param="api_key",
        code=401,
    )

async def user_api_key_auth(
    request: Request, api_key: str
) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
    users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
    if not api_key or not users_dir:
        raise _invalid_api_key()
    for user in USERS:
        try:
            with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                    return api_key
        except OSError:
            continue
    raise _invalid_api_key()
```

The class-based `CustomAuth` (and the whole `litellm.integrations.custom_auth` module) is gone; the supported contract is now a module-level **async `user_api_key_auth(request, api_key)`** function: raise to reject, return a key (or a `UserAPIKeyAuth`) to allow. That also makes my earlier `importlib` base-class fallback obsolete — it can be dropped entirely.

Two changes in `lite-llm/setup.nix`:

**1. Replace the `custom-auth` binding:**

```nix
  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are
  stored in the tool's configuration directory, one file per user,
  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os
    from typing import Union

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:
            raise invalid_api_key()
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                        return api_key
            except OSError:
                continue
        raise invalid_api_key()
  '';
```

**2. Point the YAML at the function instead of the class:**

```nix
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";
  };
```

Everything else (key generation, `LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR`, the config-dir bundle) is unchanged.

Two notes on the semantics:

- **Return value**: the docs example returns a master key because their setup has one. We have no master key and no database, so there is no further key resolution downstream — returning the presented key itself is the correct "allow" result here. If testing ever shows litellm trying to look up the returned key, the one-line alternative is `return UserAPIKeyAuth(api_key=api_key, user_id=user)`, which supplies the auth object directly.
- **Rejection**: I raise `ProxyException(code=401)` rather than a bare `Exception` so clients get a proper 401 with a JSON error body instead of an opaque 500.

**Verify** after rebuild + relaunch:

```bash
curl -s localhost:4000/v1/models            # → 401 {"error": ... "Invalid API key"}
curl -s localhost:4000/v1/models \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $(< ~/.config/development-tools-config/private/prelude-lite-llm/users/alice.key)"
# → 200, model list
```

Let me carefully reconstruct the full file:

```nix
/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are
  stored in the tool's configuration directory, one file per user,
  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os
    from typing import Union

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:
            raise invalid_api_key()
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                        return api_key
            except OSError:
                continue
        raise invalid_api_key()
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory that bundles the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the custom
  authenticator relative to the directory that contains the yaml
  configuration, so both files need to reside in the same directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user:
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and
  no authentication is enabled.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. A random
      key is generated for users that do not have one
      yet; existing keys are re-used. The directory
      holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}

/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are

  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:

  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";

  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''

    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**

  to-user-key-setup = user:

  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"

  ;

  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and

  users-setup =

    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are
  stored in the tool's configuration directory, one file per user,
  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.

  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os
    from typing import Union

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:
            raise invalid_api_key()
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                        return api_key
            except OSError:
                continue
        raise invalid_api_key()
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory that bundles the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the custom
  authenticator relative to the directory that contains the yaml
  configuration, so both files need to reside in the same directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user:
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and
  no authentication is enabled.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. A random
      key is generated for users that do not have one
      yet; existing keys are re-used. The directory
      holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}

a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**

  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:

      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.

      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";

  };

  get-provider-params = model:

    import os

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str

Full file:

/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are
  stored in the tool's configuration directory, one file per user,
  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os
    from typing import Union

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:
            raise invalid_api_key()
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                        return api_key
            except OSError:
                continue
        raise invalid_api_key()
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";
  };

  ... rest unchanged ...
```

This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are
  stored in the tool's configuration directory, one file per user,
  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os
    from typing import Union

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:
            raise invalid_api_key()
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                        return api_key
            except OSError:
                continue
        raise invalid_api_key()
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory that bundles the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the custom
  authenticator relative to the directory that contains the yaml
  configuration, so both files need to reside in the same directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user:
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and
  no authentication is enabled.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. A random
      key is generated for users that do not have one
      yet; existing keys are re-used. The directory
      holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}

/**
This module is responsible for generating all the flake attributes
that result from a lite-llm configuration. Multiple named lite-llm
configurations can be present in a flake, each will result in
a separate set of attributes in the flake.
*/
{ pkgs, lib, devtools-prelude, lite-llm-name, lite-llm, ... }:
let
  /**
  Contains llm provider specific parameters
  that are used to build the lite-llm configuration.
  This includes:
    - api_key: The provider API key. For security reasons, the
      key is not saved to the nix store. Instead each provider
      will read it from a specific environmental variable.
      When the lite-llm instance is launched, these variables
      will be set by reading the keys from a private directory.
  */
  provider-params = {
    openrouter = rec {
      key-env-name = "OPENROUTER_API_KEY";
      api_key = "os.environ/${key-env-name}";
    };
  };

  get-provider-params = model:
  let
    parts = lib.strings.splitString "/" model;
    provider = lib.elemAt parts 0;
  in
    if lib.hasAttr provider provider-params
    then provider-params.${provider}
    else throw "Unsupported model provider '${provider}'"
  ;

  /**
  Create an attribute set matching the configuration that
  lite-llm expects for each model definition.
  */
  to-model-config = model_name: { model, ... }:
  let
    provider-params = get-provider-params model;
    provider-config = { inherit (provider-params) api_key; };
  in
    {
      inherit model_name;
      litellm_params = {
        inherit model;
      } // provider-config;
    }
  ;

  /**
  The names of the users defined in the configuration. The names
  are used as file names for the generated authentication keys,
  so they are validated to only contain file-name-safe characters.
  */
  user-names =
  let
    names = lib.attrNames lite-llm.users;
    invalid = lib.filter (name: builtins.match "[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]*" name == null) names;
  in
    if invalid == [ ]
    then names
    else throw "lite-llm user names must be file-name-safe, invalid: ${toString invalid}"
  ;

  /**
  A litellm custom authenticator that validates the bearer token
  of each request against the keys of the users defined in the
  configuration. litellm 1.89.0 no longer ships the class based
  'CustomAuth' interface; the supported interface is the module
  level 'user_api_key_auth' coroutine. litellm calls it with the
  incoming request and the presented api key: raising an exception
  rejects the request, returning a key allows it. The keys are
  stored in the tool's configuration directory, one file per user,
  under the directory pointed to by the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable, which is exported by the launch script
  before litellm starts. Only the user names are embedded in the
  module, so removing a user from the configuration revokes their
  access even if their key file still exists.
  */
  custom-auth = pkgs.writeText "custom_auth.py" ''
    import os
    from typing import Union

    from fastapi import Request

    from litellm.proxy._types import ProxyException, UserAPIKeyAuth

    USERS = ${builtins.toJSON user-names}

    def invalid_api_key() -> ProxyException:
        return ProxyException(
            message="Invalid API key",
            type="invalid_request_error",
            param="api_key",
            code=401,
        )

    async def user_api_key_auth(
        request: Request, api_key: str
    ) -> Union[UserAPIKeyAuth, str]:
        users_dir = os.environ.get("LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR", "")
        if not api_key or not users_dir:
            raise invalid_api_key()
        for user in USERS:
            try:
                with open(os.path.join(users_dir, user + ".key")) as key_file:
                    if key_file.read().strip() == api_key:
                        return api_key
            except OSError:
                continue
        raise invalid_api_key()
  '';

  lite-llm-config = {
    model_list = lib.attrValues (lib.mapAttrs to-model-config lite-llm.models);
  } // lib.optionalAttrs (user-names != [ ]) {
    general_settings.custom_auth = "custom_auth.user_api_key_auth";
  };

  lite-llm-config-yml =
    pkgs.writeText "config.yml" (lib.generators.toYAML {} lite-llm-config)
  ;

  /**
  A directory that bundles the lite-llm yaml configuration together
  with the custom authenticator module. litellm resolves the custom
  authenticator relative to the directory that contains the yaml
  configuration, so both files need to reside in the same directory.
  */
  lite-llm-config-dir = pkgs.runCommand "litellm-config-${lite-llm-name}" {} ''
    mkdir -p "$out"
    cp "${lite-llm-config-yml}" "$out/config.yml"
    cp "${custom-auth}" "$out/custom_auth.py"
  '';

  /**
  The environment that provides the 'litellm' executable, the
  proxy server that serves the configured models. The nixpkgs
  'litellm' package only propagates the dependencies of the
  core library, but running the proxy server requires the
  dependencies of the 'proxy' extra, i.e. the ones that
  'pip install litellm[proxy]' would install. Those are added
  to the environment explicitly.
  */
  litellm = pkgs.python3.withPackages (ps:
    [ ps.litellm ]
    ++ ps.litellm.optional-dependencies.proxy
  );

  /**
  The list of model providers used by the models configured for
  this lite-llm instance. The list contains no duplicates.
  */
  used-providers = lib.unique (
    lib.mapAttrsToList (_: { model, ... }:
      lib.head (lib.strings.splitString "/" model)
    ) lite-llm.models
  );

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that read the API key of the given
  provider from a file located in the tool's configuration
  directory (DEVTOOLS_CONFIG) and export it as an environmental
  variable. The key is expected to be saved in a file named after
  the environmental variable that lite-llm reads it from. If the
  file does not exist, the commands inform the user where to save
  the key and exit with a non-zero status.
  */
  to-api-key-setup = provider:
  let
    params = get-provider-params provider;
    key-file = "$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/${params.key-env-name}";
  in
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "${key-file}" ]]; then
    echo -e "\033[31mError: The API key for the '${provider}' provider is missing.\033[0m" >&2
    echo "Save the key in '${key-file}' and launch '${lite-llm-name}' again." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  export ${params.key-env-name}="$(<"${key-file}")"
  ''
  ;

  api-key-setup = lib.concatStrings (map to-api-key-setup used-providers);

  /**
  Generate the shell commands that ensure that the given user has
  an authentication key. The key is stored in a file named after
  the user, located in the users directory of the tool's
  configuration directory. If the file does not exist, a random
  key is generated and saved there. Existing keys are never
  overwritten, so keys are re-used across launches.
  */
  to-user-key-setup = user:
  ''
  if [[ ! -f "$users_dir/${user}.key" ]]; then
    ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl rand -hex 32 > "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    chmod 600 "$users_dir/${user}.key"
    echo "Generated the authentication key of '${user}': $users_dir/${user}.key"
  fi
  ''
  ;

  /**
  The shell commands that prepare the authentication keys of all
  the users defined in the configuration. The directory holding
  the keys is exported through the 'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR'
  environmental variable so that the custom authenticator can
  locate it. When no users are defined the commands are empty and
  no authentication is enabled.
  */
  users-setup =
    if user-names == [ ]
    then ""
    else
    ''
    users_dir="$DEVTOOLS_CONFIG/users"
    mkdir -p "$users_dir"
    export LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR="$users_dir"
    ${lib.concatStrings (map to-user-key-setup user-names)}
    ''
  ;

  /**
  This wrapper script runs lite-llm with the configuration
  defined by the 'lite-llm-config-dir' derivation. The
  process proceeds as follows:
    1. Source the tool specific environment using the
      'devtools-prelude.get-config' function. This will
      define the DEVTOOLS_CONFIG environmental variable
      which points to the tool's configuration directory
      that will be used for storing things like provider
      API keys.
    2. Based on the llm models defined in the config,
      define the necessary environmental variables for
      the API keys. The variables will be read from files
      located in the tool's configuration directory. The
      expectation is that the user will put the api keys
      in the right files. If the file does not exist,
      exit and inform the user where to save the key.
    3. Based on the users defined in the config, ensure
      that each user has an authentication key. A random
      key is generated for users that do not have one
      yet; existing keys are re-used. The directory
      holding the keys is exported through the
      'LITE_LLM_USERS_DIR' environmental variable, which
      is read by the custom authenticator.
    4. Launch litellm with the settings specified by
      the config coming from the 'lite-llm' value.
      The structure of this object is specified
      in the ./config.nix file.
  */
  launch-lite-llm = pkgs.writeScript "launch-${lite-llm-name}" ''
    #!${pkgs.bash}/bin/bash
    set -e

    source "${devtools-prelude.get-config lite-llm-name}"

    ${api-key-setup}

    ${users-setup}

    exec ${litellm}/bin/litellm \
      --config "${lite-llm-config-dir}/config.yml" \
      --port "${toString lite-llm.port}"
  '';

in
{
  config.apps."devtools/${lite-llm-name}" = {
    type = "app";
    program = "${launch-lite-llm}";
  };
}
